市場焦點
財報密集週登場，同步關注Fed決策
美國
301關稅
• 7月24日《1974年貿易法》第122條全球10%臨時關稅到期後，川普政府改援引第301條款，
對60個經濟體課徵10%至12.5%關稅，並豁免部分原物料與能源商品 。由於部分國家商品適
用最惠國待遇（MFN），台灣、日本、南韓等實際新增關稅可能低於表面稅率 。整體而言，
新版301關稅使美國平均有效關稅僅由10.6%升至10.7%，衝擊低於市場預期。
黃金
• 本波黃金牛市的基本面主要來自烏俄衝突後的地緣政治考量 ，並疊加居高不下的美國債務壓
力，各國央行開始調節美元並加大黃金儲備的比重 。世界黃金協會調查顯示 ，多數央行仍有
意願在未來加大黃金儲備。
• 短線金價在4,000美元/盎司出現逢低買盤交易 ，本週留意美伊衝突是否持續擴大 ，若能源通

膨未超出預期，4,000美元有望成為近期關鍵支撐價位。
中國AI產業
• 中國AI新創公司月之暗面發布模型 Kimi K3，彭博分析師指出其訓練規模高達2.8兆參數，代
表對運算資源與記憶體容量需求的大幅提升 ，展望後市無論是美國或中國 AI產業，皆需持續
投入GPU、HBM記憶體及高速網路設備，以應對更複雜的模型訓練與推論，這表示市場將繼
續支撐SK海力士、台積電和輝達等公司的需求前景。

美企職場信心分歧，反映勞動市場降溫壓力
• 員工信心續降，勞動市場動能趨緩：ADP Research Institute公布7月員工動機與承諾指數（EMC Index）降至129，連
續第三個月下滑。十大產業中有六個產業走弱 ，其中教育服務業跌至2024年5月以來低點，資訊及不動產業亦同步下滑 ，
以1,000至2,499人規模企業降幅較為明顯，顯示部分服務業員工信心仍偏疲弱，勞動市場動能持續降溫。
• 製造業維持韌性，產業表現持續分化：相較之下，製造業及批發零售業員工情緒已連續三個月改善 ，反映內需與供應鏈
韌性仍支撐部分產業；重複性工作職位信心亦略有回升 ，知識型及技能型工作者則大致持平 ，顯示不同產業及職務間仍
存在結構性差異。
• 後市觀察：員工信心偏弱顯示美國就業市場正由過熱逐步回歸正常 ，短期仍須觀察企業招聘與薪資變化 。投資布局可聚

焦美國工業、自動化設備及國防航太相關產業 ，受惠製造業投資及企業提升生產力需求；另可搭配美國中小型成長股基
金，掌握景氣分化下的結構性機會。
資料來源： ADP Research Worker Sentiment Survey， 2026.7.22
員工動機與承諾指數（EMC Index）及部分分項指數走勢 依企業規模分類的EMC指數

川普政府改援引第301條款，7/24起實施新關稅
• 川普政府改援引《1974年貿易法》第301條款，7/24起實施新關稅：美國依據《1974年貿易法》第122條實施的全球10%
臨時關稅於7月24日到期。川普政府改用《1974年貿易法》第301條款以強迫勞動為由，對60個經濟體加徵10%至12.5%的
關稅，考量到物流傳播與運輸時程，針對在途貨物的關稅措施將延後至美東時間7月28日凌晨12:01正式生效，並對部分原物
料、稀土、能源、化肥及特定產品提供豁免，以降低對供應鏈與通膨的衝擊。
• 台灣、歐盟、日本、南韓、瑞士，實際新增的關稅負擔可能比其他國家小：根據最惠國待遇（Most Favored Nation, MFN）
原則，例如：台灣出口某項產品到美國，原本MFN稅率為 2.5%，若川普政府此次301措施規定「與MFN合計最高10%」，

代表美國不是再加徵10%，而是將最終關稅調整至10%，因此，這項商品實際新增的關稅只有 7.5%（10%－2.5%）。反之，
如果某項產品原本MFN稅率已達 12%，則因已高於301規定的10%，不會再額外加徵。這也是為什麼市場特別關注台灣、
日本、南韓等出口國，因為若產品原本MFN稅率已不低，實際新增的關稅負擔可能比其他國家小。
資料來源：US Trade Representative， 2026.7.24
• 白宮文件中提到的 「Net Rate（淨稅率）」：最終適用關稅 = MFN基本關稅 + 301新增關稅，上限為白宮公告的10%或12.5%。
• 加拿大：酒類、汽車與乳製品等多個產業因貿易爭端，自8/19起再加徵50%關稅。加拿大進口商品面臨的實際關稅為：301條款10%基本稅率＋50%額外關稅＋MFN基礎稅率
• 巴西：部分出口產品（如機械、服飾、乙醇等）已另加徵25%關稅。巴西進口商品面臨的實際關稅為：301條款12.5%基本稅率＋25%額外關稅＋MFN基礎稅率
301條款各國關稅稅率（7/24起）

301條款接棒，美國關稅政策延續但衝擊有限
• 301條款接棒，美國關稅政策延續但衝擊有限：隨著依據《1974年貿易法》第122條實施的臨時關稅於7月24日到期，川普
政府同步援引《1974年貿易法》第301條款，使主要貿易夥伴的關稅措施得以無縫銜接。相較於122條款具有期限限制，
301條款法律基礎更穩固，也意味著關稅政策將更具延續性，有助川普政府持續推動其貿易保護政策。
• 豁免措施降低衝擊，平均有效關稅僅微幅上升：新版301關稅整體衝擊低於市場預期。依最新估算，美國平均有效關稅稅率
僅由約10.6%微升至10.7%，增幅僅0.1%，不僅低於先前預估，也低於法院裁決前約13.5%的水準。顯示川普政府雖維持對
外強硬的貿易立場，但透過調整稅率結構與豁免範圍，在延續政策目標的同時，也避免關稅對美國經濟與企業成本造成過大

的衝擊。 301條款各國關稅稅率（7/24起）
資料來源：US Trade Representative， Bloomberg， 2026.7.24

自由現金流 -59億美元 -84億美元
AI亮點 Google Cloud 由前一季的+63%提升至+82% Data Center +59%
市場焦點
• 本業（不含投資利得）的成長依然穩健：Google Search及其他廣告營收年增
17%達633億美元，略低於預估的634億美元；YouTube廣告營收110.6億美
元、年增13%，優於預期108億美元。穩健但未全面超標。

• 資本支出上修：2026年資本支出由1,800億至1,900億美元，再度上修至2,050
億美元，高於市場原先預估約1,880億美元。
• 自由現金流由正轉負：儘管營運現金流年增41%至391億美元，仍不敵資本支
出的速度，導致單季自由現金流轉負。
• AI變現進度：Gemini 月活躍用戶已達9.5億，模型透過API每分鐘處理的
token量由前一季的160億個升至220億個；企業版Gemini Enterprise的採用
率已涵蓋近九成的Fortune 100企業。
• 獲利超過市場預期：資料中心業務是本季最大亮點，營收年增59%，成長幅
度超過整體營收增速的兩倍，反映AI伺服器需求已成為最核心的成長引擎。預
估第三季營收將落在158億至168億美元，優於法人共識的151億美元。
• 晶圓代工仍處於虧損階段：第二季財報顯示帳面GAAP淨損擴大至110億美元，
但Non-GAAP標準下淨利達22億美元。
• 資本支出上修：2026年資本支出計畫由180億美元上修至逾200億美元。
• 同業比較：台積電2026年第二季美元營收達402億美元，年增33.7%，毛利率

高達67.7%；相較之下，英特爾晶圓代工事業群同季營收57.7億美元，年增
31%，毛利率42%，但仍處於營運虧損狀態。英特爾晶圓代工目前仍處虧損
擴產階段，18A製程能否吸引更多外部客戶，將是縮小差距的關鍵之一。
小結
• 雲端成長是實打實的利多，但資本支出上修與現金流轉負令市場擔憂。AI是否
能轉化成財報上的實際營收貢獻，仍需要接下來幾季持續觀察。
• TD Cowen：目標價 475美元，買入評級
BMO Capital：目標價至 465美元，買入評級
Morgan Stanley：目標價400美元，買入評級
UBS：目標價 379美元，中立評級
• 財報與第三季展望優於預期，不過公司仍處於轉型階段，後續需持續觀察AI布
局、代工業務進展、毛利率及現金流改善。
• Citi：目標價 130美元，買入評級
UBS：目標價 121美元，買入評級
TD Cowen：目標價 115美元，買入評級

中國AI新創公司-月之暗面於7月中發布模型Kimi K3，引發市場高度關注
• 中國AI新創公司月之暗面於 7月中發布模型Kimi K3，引發市場
高度關注：由於Kimi K3在程式碼生成與代理式任務展現優異表
現，市場一度擔憂中國 AI模型進步 ，將降低企業對美國模型的
依賴度，進而影響Anthropic 、OpenAI 等業者未來擴張速度 ，
連帶衝擊輝達 、SK海力士及三星電子等 AI硬體供應鏈需求 ，因
此相關個股在消息公布後出現短期修正 。彭博分析師指出 Kimi
K3雖在性能與成本效益上具競爭力 ，但其訓練規模高達2.8兆參
數，且支援100萬Token超長上下文窗口，代表對運算資源與記
憶體容量需求的大幅提升 ，隨著中國政府持續推動開源 AI發展，
各類大型模型將加速商業化部署 ，反而有望帶動更多推論算力

需求，形成新的AI基礎建設投資循環。
• 展望後市：相較於過去中國模型主打低價策略，Kimi K3已逐步
向高性能市場靠攏 ，因此未來無論是美國或中國 AI產業，皆需
持續投入GPU、HBM記憶體及高速網路設備 ，以應對更複雜的
模型訓練與推論 ，這表示市場 將繼續支撐 SK海力士、台積電和
輝達等公司的需求前景。
美國企業使用中國AI模型之每週Token占比(OpenRouter)
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.7.23

2027年後人形機器人將趕超工業機器人成為減速器需求主力
• 2027年後人形機器人將趕超工業機器人成為減速器需求主力 ：根據產業預估，人形機器人對諧波減速器的需求將自2028年起
快速攀升，並逐步超越工業機器人成為主要需求來源 ，進一步改變諧波減速器 、行星減速器及RV減速器的市場結構 。市場規
模方面，整體減速器市場預計將由 2026年的約50億元人民幣成長至 2030年的230億元人民幣，年複合成長率CAGR超過40%。
其中，應用於高精度關節的諧波減速器成長最為顯著 ，市場規模可望由2026年的20億元人民幣大幅提升至2030年的130億元
人民幣。
• 展望後市：短期而言，工業機器人仍為諧波減速器市場的主要需求支柱；然而 ，隨著人形機器人進入量產階段 ，預估至2030

年，人形機器人相關需求將超過工業機器人的六倍以上 ，成為帶動產業成長的核心動能 。此外，中國本土廠商在諧波及 RV減
速器領域持續追趕日本領導廠商 ，技術差距逐步縮小 ，加上自動化設備升級及半導體資本支出擴張帶動需求成長 ，均有助於
推升減速器市場規模。機器人供應鏈後市看好，修正可視為中期布局窗口。。
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.7.20

海外資金持續流入美國資產，民間資本支撐美債需求
• 民間資金持續加碼，美國資產吸引力仍強：美國財政部5月TIC資料顯示，海外資金淨流入1,322億美元，其中民間資金淨
流入1,720億美元，抵銷官方部門 399億美元淨流出；外資淨買超美國長天期證券 2,628億美元，帶動長天期證券淨流入
2,327億美元，顯示全球資金仍持續配置美元資產，美國金融市場流動性維持穩健。
• 官方持債調整，民間需求成主要支撐： 日本仍為美債最大海外持有國 ，中國持有部位維持近年低檔 ，官方持債結構持續
調整；不過在殖利率維持相對高檔下 ，保險公司、退休基金及國際資產管理機構持續增加配置 ，使美債需求逐步由民間
資本接棒支撐。
• 市場看法：海外資金持續流入有助支撐美債需求及美元資產評價 ，但仍須留意聯準會政策 、財政赤字及公債供給變化 。

投資建議可考慮以5至10年期美國公債、投資等級公司債作為核心配置 ，股票則可聚焦大型AI科技股及美國大型股ETF，
兼顧收益與成長機會。
2026年美國TIC國際資本財流向變動對比 美國主要海外持債國/地區
資料來源：U.S. Department of the Treasury，2026.7.15

黃金價格短期受利率政策影響，央行買盤有望提供長期成長動能
• 短期觀察：美國新一輪核心通膨數據自近期高點回落，但在經濟成長與就業市場穩健的情境下，僅稍微收斂聯準會升息的預
期，市場基本維持年底前將升息1~2碼的預測。在此情景下，短線金價在4,000美元/盎司出現逢低買盤交易，本週留意美伊
衝突是否持續擴大，若能源通膨未超出預期，4,000美元有望成為近期關鍵支撐價位。
• 長期觀察：本波黃金牛市的基本面主要來自烏俄衝突後的地緣政治考量，並疊加居高不下的美國債務壓力，各國央行開始調
節美元並加大黃金儲備的比重。世界黃金協會調查顯示，多數央行仍有意願在未來加大黃金儲備；此外，機構調查顯示，因
儲量耗盡、礦石品位下降、老舊礦脈關閉等因素，黃金供給未來可能逐年減少，中長期供需面有望為黃金帶來慢牛行情。

• 策略：由於短線驅動黃金走勢的因素眾多，波動度可能比往年平均較高，建議投資人回歸多元資產配置的角度，逢回適度布
局，風險承受度較高的投資人，可布局在估值相對處在低檔的金礦類股。
資料來源：世界黃金協會、富蘭克林投顧，截至2025年，兆豐銀行整理。
全球黃金產量預估
資料來源：CRU Consulting(2024.12.22)，富蘭克林投顧。
全球央行黃金儲備佔比

• 財政體質穩健，政策空間相對充裕：從財政基本面觀察，美國政府財政赤字長期維持高檔，2026年政府債務占GDP比重已
達124%，財政壓力持續攀升。相較之下，新興市場整體政府債務占GDP比重約61%，僅約為美國的一半，財政體質相對穩
健，政策操作空間也更具彈性。在通膨具有黏性與經濟成長放緩並存的環境下，部分新興國家仍有能力透過擴張性財政政策
支撐內需與經濟成長，整體基本面相對具韌性。
• 評價具吸引力，宜布局美元計價新興債：從投資價值來看，新興市場債券目前評價仍具吸引力，相較美國投資級債與非投資
等級債，仍提供較高的殖利率與息差優勢。然而，在全球通膨壓力尚未完全消退下，部分新興市場貨幣仍面臨貶值風險。雖
然當地貨幣計價債券具備較高潛在報酬，但考量匯率波動可能侵蝕投資收益，現階段仍建議以美元等主流貨幣計價的新興市

場債券作為布局重心，在兼顧收益與風險控管下提升投資效率。

2. 本資料提及之經濟走勢預測不必然代表基金之績效，基金投資風險請詳閱基金公開說明書。
3. 基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益 ，投資人申購
前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 本資料所載內容如涉及商品資訊 ，商品相關資訊仍以經核可之條款及文宣 (DM)為主，本行網站已備有公開說明書可供查閱基金應負擔之費用及相關投資風險 。投
資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。
5. 本資料所載內容取自本公司相信可靠之資料來源，且為特定日期之判斷，本公司不保證其即時性、精確性及完整性，亦不提供或嘗試遊說客戶做為投資之判斷依據。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。

2  兆豐國際商業銀行http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
 美國聯邦公開市場委員會(FOMC)於 7/29以 9:3投票結果決議將利率維持在
3.50%-3.75%不變。委員會表示儘管受到中東衝突影響，不確定性仍高，惟美國
生產力成長和資本投資表現強勁，加上就業市場仍具韌性，整體經濟活動維持穩
定擴張。另目前通膨仍高於聯準會設定的2%目標水準，委員會將關注數據變化
以實現物價穩定。
 歐元區第二季GDP季增0.4%優於預期，並較去年同期成長1.0%，主要係受到
AI投資及政府支出增加所提振，抵銷部分能源成本上升的負面衝擊。此外，歐
元區7月CPI年增率自前月的2.8%上升至2.9%，歐元區經濟基本面穩健加上通
膨壓力，令市場預期歐洲央行將於9月再度升息。
 日本央行於7/31以8比1投票結果決議將利率維持在1%不變，央行總裁植田
和男於會後記者會表示，央行預計將持續升息，將密切關注中東局勢發展，並根
據經濟、物價和金融狀況的變化調整貨幣政策。
 日本及美國財政部相繼於7/30-7/31大舉購入日圓，係睽違十多年再次聯合進行

外匯市場干預，令日圓單日一度升破3%，創下近兩年最大漲幅，顯示近期日圓
大幅貶值迫使日本政府穩定匯率的行動升級。
 澳洲公布第二季CPI年增率自前值4.1%下降至4.0%，低於市場預期，主要係因
能源成本下跌，通膨放緩令市場預期澳洲央行將於下月維持利率於4.35%不變；
惟澳洲央行總裁布洛克(Michele Bullock)再度強調，短期經濟前景仍存在高度不
確定性，若通膨未明顯回落，未來仍可能再度調升利率。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢 
Aug/03/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週台幣匯價高點為32.240，低點在32.528，以32.292作收，較前一週收盤價
升值6.6分。 
上週台幣先升後貶，週初美元指數高檔震盪，台幣開低走低，加以外資持續匯
出，台幣走貶至32.450；週間，聯準會利率決議以9比3的票數維持利率不變在
3.50%~3.75%，美元指數脫離本週101.64高位，不過台幣仍受到外資持續賣超台股，
加以正值股利發放匯出旺季，台幣走貶至32.528週內低點；臨近週末，受到日圓大
幅升值帶動美元指數一度跌破100整數關口，台幣跳空開高，加以適逢月底出口商
賣美金需求增加，台幣收斂週初以來部分跌幅，終場在央行調節之下收在32.292。 
＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣區間震盪。就國際市場方面，美國公布最新6月核心PCE年增率
3.3%低於上個月的3.4%， 顯示通膨並未進一步惡化 ， 緩解聯準會現階段升息的預期 。
不過中東局勢尚未明朗，油價波動加劇，通膨仍有升溫的風險，聯準會後續仍有升
息的預期，不利台幣表現。而上週日圓大幅度升值係美國及日本當局聯手做出干預
行動，日圓升勢可能持續，有望支撐台幣走勢；就台灣市場來看，雖然外資轉為買
超台股，不過8月仍為股利發放季節，預計仍有資金匯出動能。綜合以上，預計本
週台幣區間震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：32.100-32.400

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢 
Aug/03/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣震盪走升。週初，美伊局勢反覆，外部不確定仍大，但人民幣匯率
基本保持穩定，整體波動有限，僅在6.7600~6.7700間窄幅震盪；週間，聯準會利率
決議以9比3的票數維持利率不變在3.50%~3.75%。美元指數脫離本週101.64高
位，帶動人民幣升破6.7600；臨近週末，受到日圓大幅升值帶動美元指數一度跌破
100整數關口，人民幣升破前波高點走升至6.7413週內最高。終場境外人民幣收在
6.7525，週線升值191點，境內人民幣則收在6.7525，週線升值177點。  
＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣震盪偏升。就國際市場來看，聯準會如預期維持利率不變，但
三位官員主張升息，且主席華許表示並非將決議結果視為暫停升息，顯示內部對於
通膨風險仍保持高度關注。決議出爐後，美元指數脫離近期高點，有利人民幣表現；
就中國國內市場來看 ，人行將中間價偏強設定於6.7900下方 ， 釋放人民幣偏強信號 ，
亦持續支撐人民幣走勢，不過就中國基本面來看，7月製造業PMI 49.2與非製造業
PMI 49雙雙落入50榮枯線下方，基本面表現不如預期將限制人民幣升值的速度與
幅度；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH小時圖中，短中天期均線由糾結轉為向下發
散，加以向下跌破前波低點，顯示空方動能延續。綜合以上，預估本週人民幣震盪
偏升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.7400-6.7800

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢 
         Aug/03/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週USD/JPY下跌。週初，美國暫停對伊朗空襲帶動市場風險情緒回溫，
USD/JPY下行至163.3後回升至163.7附近波動；週三FOMC宣布利率不變，美元
賣壓出籠USD/JPY回落至163.3，週四晚間日本政府進行外匯干預，USD/JPY大幅
走跌，最低來到157.96；臨近週末，日本央行利率決議宣布維持利率不變，日圓回
吐部分漲幅，USD/JPY最高回升至160.9附近，週五美國財政部亦出手支撐日圓，
USD/JPY再度大幅走跌，終場收在本週最低點157.57，週線大跌627點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週USD/JPY震盪偏弱，主要原因如下：一、日本央行上週五利率決議宣
布維持利率不變，但強調全球人工智能需求強勁加劇通膨壓力，並首次警告核心通
膨可能超過日央目標，釋放偏鷹派訊號，預期將為日圓帶來支撐。二、美國和日本
政府睽違十多年來首次聯合進行外匯干預以支撐疲軟的日圓匯率，日圓創下近兩年
最大漲幅，顯示近期日圓持續貶值迫使日本政府穩定匯率的行動升級，預期此次干
預將緩解市場做空日圓力道，限縮USD/JPY上行空間。三、就技術面而言，USD/JPY
匯價跌破月線、季線和年線關卡，原先的多頭格局已遭破壞，後續關注是否進一步
測試前波低點155整數關卡。綜合以上，預估USD/JPY本週呈現震盪偏弱。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：155.00-162.00 
前週交易區間 
157.57 163.94

6  兆豐國際商業銀行http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌歐元匯率走勢 
         Aug/03/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週EUR/USD上漲。週初，美國暫停對伊朗消息使市場風險偏好回溫，帶動
EUR/USD走升延續一度突破1.14整數關卡後回落；週間，市場等待FOMC利率決
議結果，EUR/USD大致在1.1360至1.1405間波動，週三FOMC利率決議維持不
變，帶動EUR/USD走升最高來至1.1470，此外，美國PCE數據顯示通膨降溫，使
EUR/USD上行突破1.15整數關卡最高來至1.1536；臨近週末，聯準會官員發表偏
鷹派言論使EUR/USD一度回落至1.1453後拉回，終場收在1.1527，週線上漲160
點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週EUR/USD震盪偏升，主要原因如下：一、上週公佈歐元區第二季GDP
季增0.4%優於預期，並較去年同期成長1.0%，主因為受到AI領域投資增加和充裕
的政府支出提振，抵銷美伊衝突帶來能源成本上升的影響，顯示歐元區經濟基本面
穩健；另一方面歐元區7月CPI年增2.9%高於6月的2.8%，主因為能源成本增加
10%，經濟基本面穩健及通膨壓力使市場預期歐洲央行將於9月再度升息，有利歐
元表現。二、美國和伊朗軍事衝突持續，避險需求預期將持續支撐美元需求，則將
限縮EUR/USD走升空間。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD匯價強勢突破
月線及季線以及1.15整數關卡，上週五雖一度跌破1.15但隨即拉回留下長下影線，
顯示短線多方動能較強，原先壓力已轉為支撐，後續關注是否進一步向上測試年線
1.1630。綜合以上，預估EUR/USD本週呈現震盪偏升的可能性較高。 
前週交易區間 
1.1353 1.1546

7  兆豐國際商業銀行http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1420-1.1650 
新台幣利率走勢分析

115.07.27-115.07.31 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.830%  
30天期短票利率   1.64% ~ 1.68% 
公債－10年期- A15106 1.934%~1.935% 
            － 5年期- A15108     1.82%~1.83%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分，時序邁入月底，本周又有鴻海、富邦金、聯發科、日月光
等多家大額現金股利發放，增添月底緊縮變數，帶動利率朝區間高位移動。 
週五隔夜拆款利率區間在0.805%~0.830%，30天期短票主要成交在
1.64%~1.68%，利率高位較上周上升2個基本點。 
二、 持續受上週20年公債標售需求疲弱影響，5年期指標券A15108成交利率跳
升至1.82%~1.83%，創18年來新高，10年期指標券A15106於週三等殖系統
成交在1.934%~1.935%。 
儘管聯準會維持利率不變，但受上週20年期公債標售結果不佳影響，市場氣
氛仍偏空，加上資金面偏緊及市場對央行政策轉趨緊縮的疑慮，買盤進場意願
有限，市場靜待下週10年期公債標售結果，預期短線台債殖利率仍有向上空
間，預估5年券交易區間在1.82%~1.88%、10年券交易區間在1.93%~2%。

두산에너빌리티 (034020)

Company Brief 
유틸리티/건설 
Analyst 문경원, CFA 
kyeongwon.moon@meritz.co.kr 
RA 이동석 
dongseok.lee@meritz.co.kr 2Q26 Review: 무언가 시작되는 느낌  
밥캣의 관세 환급 효과 덕분에 서프라이즈 
2Q26 연결 영업이익은 3,143억원(+15.9% YoY) 을 기록하며 컨센서스(2,629억원)
를 상회했다. 두산밥캣이 대미 상호관세 환급 효과로 영업이익이 개선(+40.5% YoY)
된 것이 주요 원인이었다. 에너빌리티 부문 영업이익 974억원(+5.4% YoY) 은 예상 
수준이었다. 수주는 4.3조원, 상반기 누적 7.1조원(+87.4% YoY) 을 달성했다. 국내
외 가스터빈 수주와 중동 가스복합 EPC 수주가 주요 동력이었다.

2026년, AP1000 발주가 시작될 가능성에 주목 
당사의 2026년 수주 전망치를 17.9조원으로 상향한다(vs 직전 보고서 15.7조원). 
이는 상반기 가스터빈 수주 호조와 AP1000 기자재 수주 가능성을 반영한 것이다. 
미국 DOE는 7월 'American Nuclear Supply Chain Loans ’을 통해 
Westinghouse에게 장납기 기자재 발주를 위한 자금을 마련해줬다. 총 10기 대형원
전을 위한 이번 자금은 단계적으로 집행된다. 당사 수주 전망치에는 2026년 중 4기
에 해당하는 장납기 기자재 발주 가능성을 반영했다. 장납기 기자재에는 원자로 및 
증기발생기가 포함된다. 협상 결과에 따라 추가적인 공급 범위(Scope) 확장도 가능
하다. 이에 더해 하반기 가스터빈 추가 수주 가능성이 높으며, SMR 역시 Nuscale 
향 수주를 연내 기대하고 있다. 수주 가이던스는 13.3조원으로 당사의 전망치와 괴리
가 있는데, 체코 두코바니 시공 수주 이후 가이던스 상향 조정 가능성이 열려있다.

대미 투자, 12차 전기본 – 주가 상승 트리거 풍부한 하반기 
투자의견 Buy, 적정주가는 145,000원에서 131,000원으로 -10% 하향한다. 장기 
마진율 조정에 따른 FCF 하락을 반영했으나 상승 여력 풍부하다. 주가 상승 트리거
로 1)수주, 2) 대미 투자, 3)12 차 전기본을 주목한다. 대미 투자는 빠르면 8월 말, 혹
은 9월 중 발표될 예정이며, 에너지 분야에서 이뤄질 전망이다. LNG, 가스 발전, 대
형원전, SMR 로 대표되는 에너지 사업 중 LNG 터미널을 제외한다면 동사가 참여가 
가능한 분야다. 당사는 대미 투자 유력 프로젝트로 SMR을 주목하는데, 만약 실현된
다면 대미 투자는 SMR 산업이 GW 급 양산을 시작하는 큰 분기점이 될 수 있다. 
이와 함께 Nuscale 향 기자재 발주도 연내 기대하고 있다. 또한 연말 전후 발표가 
예상되는 12차 전기본에서는 신규 원전 건설 계획 포함 가능성이 높다(3대 메가 프
로젝트 등 전력 수요 급증 때문). 이 역시 투자 심리를 개선시키는 요인이다.

Meritz Research  2026. 7. 28

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준) 
적정주가 (12개월) 131,000원

### Page 1

Alphabet 拟发债筹资最⾼ 250 亿美元，触发⻓端美债收益率⾛⾼与收益率曲线陡化。此次
债券发⾏的认购订单规模约达 1150 亿美元，超过预期发⾏规模的四倍以上。此次发债认购
热度超越了近期亚⻢逊和 SpaceX 等公司的债券发⾏， Alphabet 此笔交易向投资者提供了相
对较⾼的发⾏让步。
摘要：
Alphabet 正寻求通过新⼀轮美元投资级债券发⾏筹资最多 250 亿美元，此举不仅考验投资者对 AI
相关债务的真实胃⼝，更直接推动⻓端美债收益率⾛⾼，令市场承压。
据报道 6 ⽇援引知情⼈⼠透露，Alphabet 此次债券发⾏分拆为 10 个部分，期限覆盖 2 年⾄ 40 年，
最⻓期限档的初始价格指导利差约为⾼于美国国债 155 个基点。最终发⾏规模尚未确定。
受上述消息影响，美债收益率全线⾛⾼ 3 ⾄ 4 个基点，⻓期债券领跌， 10 年期美债收益率升⾄约
4.65% ， 2s10s 、 5s30s 期限利差均扩⼤⾄盘中最阔⽔平。
此次债券发⾏的认购订单规模约达 1150 亿美元，超过预期发⾏规模的四倍以上。这表明在近期

抛售后，投资者对与⼈⼯智能热潮相关的债券需求重新回暖。
此次需求超过了亚⻢逊和 SpaceX 等公司近期⼈⼯智能相关债券发⾏的认购热度。 Alphabet 此笔
交易向投资者提供了相对较⾼的发⾏让步。
此次发债的时间节点颇为敏感。就在约两周前，Alphabet 将 2026 年资本开⽀预期上调⾄最⾼
2050 亿美元，超过 2025 年⽀出规模的两倍，令市场对 AI 投资回报的疑虑重燃。
本次债券发⾏启动后， Alphabet 今年 2 ⽉发⾏的债券利差扩⼤。截⾄美国东部时间上午 10:28 ，
与周三收盘⽔平相⽐，利差扩⼤了 6-12 个基点。
与此同时，短端美债亦受另⼀重压⼒：据英国《⾦融时报》报道，美联储主席沃什若在未来⼏
周看到偏热通胀数据，将准备在 9 ⽉会议上加息，报道发布后短期美债收益率同步⾛⾼。
AI 债券需求降温，市场承接压⼒上升

Alphabet 此次发债，是在 AI 基础设施融资需求与投资者信⼼出现裂痕的背景下推进的。上周，⼀
家与⻉莱德关联的公司为 Meta Platforms 位于德克萨斯州的数据中⼼项⽬发⾏了 125 亿美元债
券，初始需求冷淡，此前亚⻢逊的相关债券发⾏同样遭遇认购热情不⾜。包括 SpaceX 在内的多
家企业新发 AI 相关债券，⼆级市场利差亦出现⾛阔。
2025 年以来，⼤型科技公司通过债券市场累计融资逾 3500 亿美元，其中 Alphabet 与亚⻢逊居于
领跑位置。Alphabet 仅在 2026 年上半年便已发债逾 500 亿美元，发⾏币种涵盖瑞⼠法郎、英镑、
欧元、加元及⽇元等多种货币，并于约两个⽉前完成近 850 亿美元的股票增发。
融资规模持续扩张，现⾦流⾸现负值

现⾃ IPO 以来⾸个⾃由现⾦流为负的季度，市场对超⼤规模科技公司 " 负现⾦流时代提前到来 " 的
担忧由此加剧。
Alphabet 同步提交申请，拟发⾏最多 400 亿美元的 A 类和 C 类股票，进⼀步扩充资本储备，为持
续扩张的 AI 基础设施投⼊提供弹药。
在债券市场， Alphabet 发债计划与美联储潜在加息预期的叠加，构成了当⽇美债承压的双重驱
动。⻓端收益率受供给压⼒推升更为显著，带动收益率曲线整体陡化。利率市场⽬前仍计⼊约
15 个基点的 9 ⽉加息预期，⾄年底累计加息预期约达 33 个基点，市场对货币政策路径的不确定性
仍⾼。
对于固定收益投资者⽽⾔，如何在 AI 资本开⽀扩张带来的供给冲击与潜在利率上⾏⻛险之间寻求
平衡，正成为当前不得不⾯对的核⼼命题。

MACRO STRATEGY
Still Waiting for Europe: Two Years Post-Draghi Report
Arianna Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
“It’s ‘do this’ or it’s a slow agony.”  – Mario Draghi
So said the former European Central Bank president upon the release of his landmark “Draghi
report” in September 2024. In the report, Draghi laid out over 380 recommendations for how
Europe could reignite growth and avoid “slow agony” in the face of fracturing geopolitics,

technological change and fierce competition from overseas.
Nearly two years later, the progress report isn’t pretty. The latest analysis from the European
Policy Innovation Council (EPIC) suggests that just 15.7% of Draghi’s recommendations have
been implemented as of July 2026, a rather modest improvement from 11.2% a year ago.
Some progress has been born of necessity, such as phasing out natural gas imports from
Russia or spending more on defense. The more difficult reforms—stronger single market

integration, unified capital markets, large-scale private investment, reducing critical material
dependencies—are slow going. As EPIC concludes in its report, “the update confirms that
Europe remains better at building legal architecture than at forcing market outcomes.”
Those are tough words for a continent battling to maintain economic influence. According to
figures from the International Monetary Fund, the EU and the UK (EU28) today make up 21%

of global gross domestic product (GDP)—or roughly half the combined share of the U.S. (26%)
and China (17%). Twenty years ago, the order looked different: EU28 accounted for 30% of
world output, while the U.S. accounted for 27% and China a mere 5%. America’s share of the
global pie has remained resilient in the face of China’s rise over the last two decades. Europe’s
share? Not so much.
Chief among the forces working against Europe in recent years has been so-called “China

Shock 2.0”. Unlike the first “China shock”, which featured a surge in low-value merchandise
exports (textiles, apparel, furniture, etc.) following China’s accession to the World Trade
Organization in 2001, the latest onslaught of exports has targeted both low-value and high-
value goods (electric vehicles, batteries, advanced machinery, electronics, etc.). And while
America’s manufacturing sector was the primary victim of China Shock 1.0, Europe has

emerged as the poster child of China Shock 2.0.
Case in point: China’s widening trade surplus with the EU, now running at an annual pace
of $326 billion. That’s more than double where it was entering this decade. In part due to
protectionist measures from the U.S., China’s overflow of goods has increasingly been routed
to Europe and the rest of the world—so much so that, for the first time in history, China’s
trade surplus with the EU has surpassed its surplus with the U.S. (Exhibit 1). For comparison, at

the start of the decade, China’s surplus with the U.S. was nearly double its surplus with the EU.
Exhibit 1: China Shock 2.0 is All About Europe.

Investment Implications
We continue to prefer the U.S. and
Emerging Markets over International
Developed Equities. Manufacturing-
led economies in Europe remain
exposed to competition from China in
key industries. Still, European markets
can provide strategic exposure to
Value and diversification away from
mega-cap Technology stocks in
portfolios.

### Page 3

3 of 8 August 3, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Indeed, just as a flood of cheap imports from China gutted America’s manufacturing base
in the early-to-mid 2000s, Europe’s manufacturing sector is feeling the pain of low-cost
competition from China in everything from electric vehicles to chemicals to pharmaceuticals.
Compared to China’s near-30% share of global manufacturing output, the EU28's share has
stagnated at roughly 18% of the global total, while Germany’s has declined to 5% (Exhibit 2).

Meanwhile, Chinese brands are gaining share where Europe cares most: in autos, for example,
brands home to Beijing captured a record 10.9% of Europe’s new car sales in June after out-
selling Japan brands in Europe for the first time in May.
1 Surveys of European auto suppliers
haven’t been promising, either, with just 10% planning to increase production capacity in
Western Europe over the next five years versus 49% in North America and 35% in China,

according to the European Association of Automotive Suppliers and McKinsey

And China’s not the only culprit. Yes, huge government subsidies have turbocharged China’s
expansion across industries, with the Organisation for Economic Co-operation and Development
estimating in June that subsidies alone explain nearly 60% of China firms’ gain in global
market share between 2005 and 2023. But a recent report from the European Trade Union
Confederation points also to the more general “financialization” of Europe’s largest companies—

i.e., reorienting away from long-run productive investment in favor of short-run shareholder
value. Owing in part to Europe’s corporate tax system, corporations have increasingly absorbed
profits into corporate balance sheets rather than recycling capital back into production and the
broader economy. In fact, since 2009, the EU’s non-financial corporate sector has saved more
than it’s invested, with net lending by corporations reaching 1.2% of the EU’s GDP in 2024.

2
Add low productive domestic investment to intense competition from China, and the case for
Europe as a region ripe for future growth becomes a hard sell. Not helping matters: outside
of its monopoly on extreme ultraviolet lithography (for now), the region has proven a relative
bystander in the AI race while competition between frontier models out of the U.S. and China
intensifies. Perhaps Brussels-based think tank Bruegel put it best: “Europe is, in effect, an

indispensable input supplier to a race in which it does not itself compete.”
All the reasons above support our tactical underweight to International Developed markets.
Europe’s bias toward sectors like Financials, Industrials and Healthcare can still be a helpful
diversifier in portfolios in our view, particularly as Technology now accounts for 37% of the S&P
500 and 41% of the MSCI Emerging Markets Index versus 9% of the MSCI Europe Index. But
with China Shock 2.0 intensifying, ongoing vulnerabilities to energy shocks, lagging productivity,

and limited AI innovation, our preference for the U.S. over the old continent remains unchanged.
Two years after Mario Draghi’s landmark report, we’re still waiting for Europe
1 European Automobile Manufacturers’ Association.
2 For more, see “Labour Squeezed, Investment Stalled: The Hidden Cost of Financialisation in Europe’s Corporate Sector” from the
European Trade Union Confederation.

4 of 8 August 3, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
No More Surprises—Six “Surprises” That Shouldn’t Surprise Investors
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Investors have had to navigate various crosscurrents over the past few years, ranging from
unsettled geopolitics, to fractured global supply chains, to disruptions from AI. Each one of
these forces—and others—has triggered a chorus of worry among market pessimists. Yet

amid the gloom, something curious keeps happening—the dire predictions of the worrywarts
never happen, disappointing and “surprising” market bears. From our vantage point, successful
investing requires distinguishing between genuine surprises and developments that merely
surprise those bearish on the U.S. In that spirit, we briefly outline six so-called “surprises” that
shouldn’t surprise investors.
Surprise #1: The U.S. Consumer Refuses to Quit. To the “surprise” of many investors,

U.S. consumers continue to spend—even in the face of rising inflation, higher interest rates,
exploding credit card balances, debilitating levels of student loan debt and shaky consumer
confidence. All these factors have lent support to those worrying about a consumer-led
recession. However, what the bears miss is this: The reservoir of U.S. consumer purchasing
power remains immense, backstopped by rising home values, appreciating financial assets,

solid wage gains and a dynamic labor market. Yes, higher energy prices have caused
consumers to trade down on products and services. And macro shocks cause consumers to
postpone discretionary purchases but spending on services and essentials continues. This
adaptability, along with exceptional underlying wealth, has long made betting against the
American consumer a losing proposition.
Surprise #2: Higher Oil Prices No Longer Break America. Yes, crude oil prices matter, but

they matter less in a U.S. economy that is more energy-efficient and less energy-dependent on
foreign supplies. We are a long way from the 1970s and 1980s, when the U.S. was a significant
importer of energy and thus vulnerable to repeated oil shocks that acted as a massive tax on
households and businesses. Thanks to the shale revolution, the U.S. is now one of the world’s
largest energy producers. This serves as a buffer to volatility in the Middle East, as well as a

cushion to growth since when energy prices rise in the U.S., massive segments of the domestic
economy—from Texas oil fields to infrastructure and equipment suppliers—thrive. Meanwhile,
energy represents a much smaller share of household spending than it did decades ago.
Modern vehicles are dramatically more fuel efficient, as is the U.S. economy, which generates
more output from services, software, healthcare, finance and technology than from energy-

intensive manufacturing. Higher oil prices still hurt consumers at the pump, but it is important
to remember—or not be surprised—that America has become significantly less vulnerable to
oil shocks due to a more diversified economy and expanding domestic energy production.
Surprise #3: The U.S. Dollar Remains King. Writing the obituary for the U.S. dollar is
almost a cottage industry in the U.S., so imagine the “surprise” every time the greenback
bounces back or becomes a safe-haven asset for investors in times of trouble. The dollar’s

world reserve status continues due in large part to the fact that the U.S. possesses the world’s
largest government bond market, deepest capital markets, strongest property rights, and
broadest range of investable assets. The buck is also backstopped by the largest and most
resilient economy in the world, which maintains a steady inflow of foreign capital into U.S.
securities. Global trade, commodities, banking and financial contracts remain overwhelmingly

denominated in dollars because the participants trust the institutions behind the currency.
Meanwhile, Europe is weighed down by structural demographic challenges, sluggish growth,
and strict regulations. China faces deep real estate vulnerabilities, regulatory unpredictability,
and maintains a closed capital account. And the emerging markets carry volatile currency risks
and political instability. The upshot for investors: stay long U.S. dollar-denominated assets and

don’t be “surprised” by the enduring strength and attractiveness of the greenback.
Surprise #4: America’s Budget Deficit Has Not Triggered Financial Armageddon.
Trillion-dollar deficits, mounting debt and debt service levels, and relentless congressional
borrowing continue to generate anxiety among investors and for good reasons. Deficits
matter. But what matters more is America’s ability and capacity to service its deficit and
debt, and on this basis, the U.S. is not staring down the barrel of financial Armageddon.

Investment Implications
We maintain our overweight to U.S.
Equities and U.S. dollar-denominated
assets in portfolios owing to
America’s unmatched capacity
to drive productivity, embrace
innovation, and absorb macro shocks.
We’d use any bouts of volatility to
add on weakness given still-strong
fundamentals.

### Page 5

5 of 8 August 3, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
The U.S. possesses financing advantages unique to the rest of the world—it borrows in its 
own currency, its debt market remains the global benchmark for safety and liquidity, and 
Treasurys remain the world’s preferred reserve asset. In addition, America’s asset base is 
extraordinary: the economy remains the largest and most innovative in the world, supported 
by abundant natural resources, world-class universities, and an unmatched entrepreneurial 
ecosystem. All the above provides the U.S. the time, flexibility and resilience to run budget 
deficits—to the “surprise” of investors. On the assumption that U.S. nominal GDP , corporate 
profits, productivity, and tax revenues continue to grow faster than the government’s financing 
obligations, the debt markets are likely to tolerate U.S. deficit/debt levels that would be 
unsustainable almost anywhere else. 
Surprise #5: U.S. Corporate Earnings Keep Exceeding Expectations. If there is one 
forecast that consistently “surprises” investors to the upside, it is the earnings power of 
Corporate America. Nearly every economic slowdown, interest-rate jolt, geopolitical crisis 
or policy scare is accompanied by downside profit warnings. Yet time after time, Corporate 
America has proven to be remarkably adept at exceeding expectations. That’s not surprising 
since U.S. firms are some of the most adaptive in the world. Faced with rising costs, firms 
automate, adopt new technologies, restructure operations, reskill workers, streamline supply 
chains, reprice products and services, and relentlessly pursue productivity gains. Standing still 
isn’t an option, nor is hoping for a government bailout or a favorable line of credit. In the U.S. 
ecosystem, you either adapt or die. The result is a corporate sector that continuously reinvents 
itself faster than analysts revise their forecasts. Just as important, the composition of corporate 
earnings has changed over the decades. Today’s largest companies derive significant value from 
software, data, intellectual property, brands, platforms and networks rather than heavy physical 
assets. These allow for scale, competitive moats, and higher profit margins. This structural shift 
has increased the resilience of corporate profits, to the “surprise” of many investors. 
Surprise #6: America Remains One of the Most Shock-Resistant Economies in the 
World. The greatest “surprise” of all to investors is how well the U.S. economy has been able to 
absorb various macro shocks that have sunk, derailed or structurally impaired other economies. 
A pandemic, a ground war in the heart of Europe, sweeping U.S. tariffs on the global trading 
system, the closure of the Strait of Hormuz—what hasn’t been thrown at the economy and 
capital markets this decade? And yet, owing to America’s capacity to drive productivity, embrace 
innovation and relentlessly reinvent itself, U.S. nominal GDP thus far in the 2020s has increased 
a staggering $10.5 trillion since the start of the decade. Meanwhile, as Exhibit 3 highlights, 
notwithstanding various macro shocks and jolts since 2020, the S&P 500 has continued to rise 
and climb—the direction, as we like to say, has been up and to the right. As of the end of July 
2026, the S&P has managed a total return of 155%, which equates to annualized returns of 
15.3%, above the historic average of 11.7% going back to 1945. Today, the ability to absorb 
shocks is all the more relevant as the markets question the credibility of the Warsh-led Federal 
Reserve (Fed) following last week’s Federal Open Market Committee meeting. Volatility and 
uncertainty have become near-term market staples. But that said, don’t be “surprised” to see 
the market adjust and reset and continue to grind higher over the long run.

THE TICKER TAPE
Is the semiconductor and broader AI buildout chain capital investment
cycle rolling over, and have we experienced the top in the cycle?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Officer
We don't believe the latest sharp downdraft across the semiconductor and AI buildout chain is
a sign that the top from June is in. The components needed to acquire the necessary compute
and power are still in short supply, while the capital investment spend is still increasing. Secondly,

this area of the market has traditionally experienced corrections during "speed bump" periods as
the computing innovation adjusts. Building the infrastructure as the innovation transitions from
training to agentic to quantum and physical AI could enter periods that require tactical shifts.
Ultimately, growth rates remain high as shortages in key areas still exist across the entire supply
chain. Execution of this entire process given the coordination needed can have speed bumps. Do

the speed bumps turn into major slowdowns and/or recessions? They can, but this is typically
late cy
cle not early to mid as we are currently, in our view. In order to assess the magnitude of
risk to the growth outlook, we will continue to closely watch both the associated spreads of debt
fin
ancing that has been increasing as well as the insurance cost of protecting against defaults
(credit default swaps–CDS) across the AI space in the coming months, part

icularly as longer
dated Treasury yields pick up (as of July 29, 30-Year Treasury yields are now at their highest
lev
els since 2007). Finally, we believe the sharp downdraft across the semiconductor and AI
buildout chain was also exacerbated by a clearing event of over-levered investors that may have
had to sell indiscriminately as the momentum in the space gathered steam in the past one to
two weeks. Clearing events can help establish a more normal path forward in which investors

begin to focus back on the fundamentals and less on trading and technicals.
As of late July, according to BofA Global Research, The MSCI Global Semiconductor Index had
fallen more than 20% from recent June highs. Performance following past pullbacks since 1999
i
n global semis differ greatly depending on whether a recession follows. After previous pullbacks
of similar magnitude, BofA Global Research also found that the average 12-month return was

44% when recession was avoided and 12% when a recession ensued. U.S. semis performed even
b
etter when a recession was avoided, rising 49%, but gained only 1% when a recession followed.
None of the global cy
cle indicators show signs of an impending downturn. The signal from the
Global Wave3 is positive for the sixth month. The global earnings revision ratio improved to 1.0,
and, for global semis, the ratio sits at a four-and-a-half year high of 1.41. That said, recovery could

take some time. On average, it took eight months for semis to recover the 14% pullback, seven
months when recessions did not follow. Given the latest forward guidance by key leaders in the AI
buildout space, we expect this cycle's recovery to be quicker than those experienced in the past.
Overexposure to the semiconductor and capital equipment space became extended at the end of
June, and this latest correction is more a sign of derisking than a sign of a large scale slow down.

July 20, 2026 
Current forecasts point to one of the strongest El Nino events on record in Q4. 
That matters because El Nino events can act as a multi-dimensional supply 
shock, with a causal link to higher food and energy prices, alongside broader 
supply-chain disruption. This would be problematic at any point in time, but it’s a 
particular issue today because of existing supply-chain stresses and the 
backdrop of above-target inflation. So a very strong El Nino event would be 
another negative supply shock, at a point when the global economy has limited 
room to absorb one. 
This paper explores what an El Nino is, and why a very strong one later this year 
would be so concerning. It also takes a look at what happened on previous 
occasions, and the channels through which it affects the broader economy and 
financial markets. 
We also consider the potential consequences this time round. Much will be 
weather dependent, but the precedents from the 1970s show how an El Nino 
event can coalesce with other shocks to keep inflation persistently above target, 
just as we’ve seen in recent years. Moreover, this is particularly damaging for 
many EM economies, where food can make up as much as a third of consumer 
expenditure.

El Nino: The next supply shock?

### Page 2

20 July 2026
Thematic Research
Page 2 Deutsche Bank AG
What is an El Nino?
n An El Nino event is the warm phase of the El Nino-Southern Oscillation
(ENSO), which is a variation in winds and sea surface temperatures in the
Pacific Ocean. This oscillates between warming phases (called El Nino) and
cooling phases (called La Nina).
n The warming El Nino phase occurs when there are unusually warm sea
surface temperatures in the eastern Pacific. These warmer waters mean

that the Pacific jet stream moves south relative to normal, which creates
changes in weather patterns and ecosystems.
n We can see the effect on global temperatures too. Even as temperatures
have ben rising anyway over recent decades, the record years have often
coincided with El Nino events, including 1998, 2016, and 2024.
n Unfortunately, El Nino events are correlated with a higher frequency of
natural disasters, with more people affected by disasters in El Nino years

relative to neutral years. Flooding is one that sees a particular increase, but
it is also associated with temperature changes too.
n The effects of an El Nino vary considerably by region. In the US, it normally
coincides with wetter conditions in the southern US along the Gulf Coast.
In stronger episodes, this can extend to California and the Southwest too.
But in other regions it is more likely to cause drought, like Australia, whilst
India sees weaker monsoon rains.

n In practical terms the main effect of an El Nino is disruption to food
production. But there are other effects too. El Ninos have been correlated
with the spread of climate-sensitive diseases, and one study found that the
El Nino in 2015 contributed to the spread of the Zika virus since it created
conditions beneficial for mosquito-borne transmission. 1  Furthermore,
natural disasters like flooding increase the frequency of poor hygiene
conditions or undernutrition, so there is an impact there too, and El Ninos

have been linked to many famines and even conflicts.
n For markets, an El Nino is capable of hitting agricultural production,
hydropower, shipping, and even has effects on public health and political
stability. In other words, it operates as a multi-channel supply shock.

What’s particularly bad about this one?
El Nino cycles typically repeat every few years with irregular frequency. However,
today’s episode is particularly concerning because forecasts suggest it will be a
very strong one, ranking among the most serious in recent history.

A very strong El Nino means it’s more likely to shift rainfall and temperature patterns
across multiple regions simultaneously. And from a macro perspective, the risks are
non-linear. For instance, a modest food shock can be absorbed via inventories,
imports or substitution. But a large shock propagating across multiple countries
can’t be absorbed as easily, with second-round inflation effects more likely to result.

The timing is particularly bad right now, because the global economy is already
facing significant supply-chain stress. Most notably, the closure of the Strait of
Hormuz has led to a spike in oil and gas prices this year. And even if the El Nino is
temporary, the resulting inflation shock will push inflation higher at a time when it’s
Figure 3: US Climate Prediction Center forecasts (%) for El Nino

### Page 4

20 July 2026
Thematic Research
Page 4 Deutsche Bank AG
already above target in many countries.
These risks are already being considered by policymakers in decision making,
particularly in emerging markets. Indeed, this year has seen central banks in India,
Peru and Colombia all reference El Nino in official publications.
What happened on previous occasions?
2023-24: Supply-chain disruption and record temperatures occurred with the last
El Nino.
n The most recent El Nino offered a clear example of what can happen, and

this didn’t even hit the “very strong” threshold.
n 2024 was the warmest year on record, and the episode saw severe
droughts in parts of the world, including parts of South America, along with
severe flooding in East Africa and South America, consistent with the
typical redistribution of rainfall during El Nino.
n Meanwhile, the Panama Canal had its 3rd driest year on record in 2023,
meaning that authorities had to cut shipping passing through. The

disruption to commercial shipping demonstrated how El Nino is more than
just a food price story.
2014-16: The last “very strong” El Nino had broader food security and public
health implications
n Just as in 2024, the El Nino of 2016 also saw the biggest upward global
temperature anomaly to date.
n Ethiopia suffered its worst drought in decades, leaving millions of people in
need of food assistance. Moreover, the Pacific hurricane seasons saw 16
hurricanes in each of 2014 and 2015, the joint-highest number of record.

n One study found that El Nino was a contributing factor to the spread of the
Zika virus, since it created conditions beneficial for mosquito-borne
transmission.
1997-98: The “very strong” El Nino also had major climactic and public health
implications
n 1998 was another record year for global temperatures, and included many
extremes around the world. For instance, San Francisco saw its wettest
rainfall season since 1889-90, whilst Peru saw 16 times its average rainfall.

n Meanwhile, after major flooding in east Africa, there was an outbreak of Rift
Valley fever in Kenya, Somalia and Tanzania. The outbreak also led to major
losses of livestock too.
1982-83: The El Nino occurs when high inflation is still lingering
n By 1982-83, inflation had fallen substantially from its earlier heights, aided
by monetary tightening like that seen under Paul Volcker at the Fed.
However, the El Nino didn’t help matters, as it caused major climactic

disruption once again, including flooding in the United States , and
droughts in Indonesia and Australia.
1972-73: The El Nino played a role in raising inflation before the first oil shock

### Page 5

20 July 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 5
n Although the oil shock of 1973 was the primary factor that drove the
stagflation of the 1970s, several other shocks including the El Nino had
already occurred beforehand. This was significant because collectively,
they served to push up inflation and inflation expectations. For instance, US
fiscal spending had already risen in the late-1960s, thanks to the Johnson
Administration’s Great Society programmes and the Vietnam War. Then in

1971, President Nixon ended the dollar’s link to gold, ending the system of
fixed exchange rates that had prevailed after WWII, and in February 1973
there was a dollar devaluation. Amidst all these inflationary pressures, the
El Nino event further drove up food prices.
Even further back
n Back in the mid-17th century, there was a period when El Nino events
happened about twice as often on average. This was an unusually turbulent
period of global history that’s been termed the “General Crisis”. Some

historians have proposed that climactic changes such as El Nino played a
role in fomenting instability.
How does an El Nino affect the economy and global
markets: The main transmission channels
A useful way to think about El Nino is a set of different supply shocks via multiple
channels: food prices; energy prices; supply chains; EM balance sheets; and
growing risk premia resulting from the heightened risk of social/political unrest. For
individual economies, the impact depends on their exposure to food in the

consumer basket, and the extent of intervention from policymakers, be that via food
subsidies or tighter monetary policy to counteract inflation.
1. Food: The clearest inflation channel
n Direct supply hit: El Nino shifts rainfall patterns, raising the odds of drought
in parts of Asia and Australia, and flooding in parts of the Americas. This
causes damage to crops, kills livestock, and leads to shipping disruption.
n Emerging market exposure : The impact of a food shock varies

considerably across different economies. In the US for instance, food-at-
home makes up just 8% of the consumer basket for CPI inflation. By
contrast, in several EM economies, over a third of household expenditure is
on food, and an IMF paper2 found that the inflation response to El Nino was
biggest in economies where food made up a higher share of the consumer
basket. So emerging markets like India and Indonesia are among those
more severely affected. Moreover, food inflation is a problematic category

because it’s a necessity, and consumers can’t easily substitute away from
it, as they can with luxury goods.
n Second-round effects: Beyond the immediate impact, El Nino also has
second round effects on inflation. For instance, governments may cushion
the impact of higher food prices with subsidies. That might reduce the
domestic hit temporarily, but by further boosting demand and raising fiscal
deficits, it pushes the adjustment elsewhere and risks creating further

inflation overall.
2. Energy and Hydropower: The less-obvious inflation channel
2 IMF Working Paper: Fair Weather or Foul? The Macroeconomic Effects of El Niño (2015), Cashin,
Mohaddes and Raissi.

### Page 6

20 July 2026
Thematic Research
Page 6 Deutsche Bank AG
n Hydropower shortfall: Lower rainfall in some regions means there’s less
electricity generated from hydro-electric dams. Moreover, the droughts
mean this cut to electricity generation is happening just as heat is raising
demand for cooling, and more water is required for irrigation. So there’s
less supply of energy, just as demand goes up.
n Fuel-switching channel: With hydropower output down, this then forces a

substitution into other forms of energy, such as gas, coal or oil-fired
generation, again raising demand for those other fuels and lifting power
prices.
3. Shipping/supply chains
n Climate effects on supply chains: The last El Nino in 2023-24 showed how
it can impact supply chains, quite aside from a direct impact on the food
supply. For instance, Panama had its third-driest year on record in 2023,
with rainfall around 30% below average, forcing authorities to restrict the

number of ships passing through. This is a point covering around 5% of
global maritime trade.
n Interaction with other chokepoints : Any supply-chain issues are
particularly troublesome today, because the Strait of Hormuz has seen
traffic almost completely stop thanks to the US-Iran conflict. So the more
transit points that are impacted, the more difficult it is for other routes to
absorb any disruption. Indeed, some measures of shipping rates have

already doubled since the start of the year
n Macro Transmission: If key routes are restricted or closed, then that raises
shipping rates further, increases delivery times, and means firms want to
hold more inventories against future disruption. So it’s another negative
supply shock, on top of that seen from higher food and energy prices.

4. EM vulnerability
n Climate impact: By virtue of their geographic position, emerging markets
are more likely to face the impact of flooding or drought associated with El
Nino.
n Household exposure: Emerging market economies have more exposure to

food prices, so any price shock hits them harder, with food making up over
a third of consumer expenditure in several countries.

### Page 7

20 July 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 7
n Policy challenges: This vulnerability makes the policy response to an El
Nino event particularly acute. Central banks might raise rates to keep
inflation expectations in check and defend the currency, but that doesn’t
solve the actual supply shock itself. Moreover, if fiscal policy is used to
cushion the impact, the shock is then shifted onto public balance sheets,
and sovereign risk can become an issue.

5. Social/political unrest
n Higher food prices raise the risk of unrest: Throughout history, higher food
prices have often been correlated with political unrest. Clearly it takes many

factors to spark unrest, but the squeeze on disposable incomes means that
any existing dissatisfaction is magnified. If an El Nino leads to higher food
prices, that in turn makes unrest more likely.
n Recent precedents: In 2010-11, higher food prices were regarded as a
contributing factor behind the Arab Spring. A few years earlier in 2007-08,
there was a wave of instability across many EM countries, including Tunisia,
Egypt, Cameroon, Ivory Coast, and Haiti. And more recently, the 2022 price

shock was a contributor to protests in Ecuador, Sierra Leone, Haiti and
Panama.
n Historical precedents : Looking further back, several of history’s most
important revolutions took place against the backdrop of higher food
prices. The French Revolution of 1789 came after a succession of harvest
failures. The 1848 revolutions in Europe happened at the end of the so-
called “Hungry Forties”, when there was a severe famine after the failure of
potato crops. And in Russia in 1917, food shortages played a key role in

fomenting discontent, with supplies hampered in part by the reallocation of
farm workers into the military for WWI.

What does it mean for economies and markets?
A very strong El Nino event would be another negative supply shock when the
global economy has limited room to absorb one. The combination of higher food

and energy prices will mechanically raise inflation, and broader supply-chain
disruption risks creating further price pressures when the Strait of Hormuz has
already been blocked.
This is particularly problematic, because experience shows that a prolonged period
of above-target inflation raises the risk of inflation expectations becoming
unanchored. That in turn can create a self-fulfilling wage-price spiral, as if firms and
individuals expect high inflation, then they will set their prices and bargain for

wages accordingly.
In several respects, this is reminiscent of the experience in the 1970s. Back then, a
series of seemingly unconnected supply shocks (including an El Nino) occurred that
collectively kept inflation high. Even though each might have been temporary on
paper, implying policy should look through and not over-react, the reality was that
the shocks began to feel permanent because they kept on happening. So inflation
expectations rose and policy eventually had to react.

Emerging markets are particularly vulnerable as well, with the risk greatest in
places where food is a larger part of the consumer basket, and where fiscal space
is more limited to accommodate any shock. Moreover, we know from recent history
that higher food prices are also linked to higher instances of social unrest, creating
further economic uncertainty.
Finally, there is a clear human cost to severe El Nino events. The frequency of natural
disasters goes up and there is a greater risk of famine. Moreover, public health

issues are often correlated with El Nino events, with several channels in operation,
including the heightened risk of cholera after flooding, or the added risk of malaria
from conditions more favourable for mosquitos.
Given the forecasts for the strength of this El Nino, this will be an important event
to look out for over the months ahead.

Document 271: FOMC uncertainty to continue testing markets (2026-08-09)

Source PDF: all report pdf/FOMC uncertainty to continue testing markets (2026-08-09).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-08-09
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

#fomc #usd #us
FOMC uncertainty to continue testing markets
By Elliot Clarke
Head of International Economics, Westpac
08:24 July 30 2026 Share
Real data is unlikely to be decisive for policy in coming months, leaving market
expectations to determine financial conditions.
At their July meeting, the FOMC voted 9-3 to leave the fed funds rate unchanged. Productivity growth
and capital investment were characterised as “strong” and overall economic momentum “solid” despite

considerable uncertainty. Labour demand and supply were assessed to be in balance, the

### Page 2

Related articles
unemployment rate having “changed little”.
“Inflation [meanwhile] remains elevated…  in part reflecting supply shocks that have driven price
increases in certain sectors”. In both the statement and the press conference, the FOMC and Chair
Warsh were resolute in their determination to bring inflation sustainably back to the 2.0%yr target.
However, also apparent is that, at this juncture, it is unclear how far price shocks are permeating
through the consumer basket. This applies to the current volatility in energy prices, due to the conflict in
the Middle East, and the ongoing price surge for tech components. It will take many months to
determine the full implications for underlying inflation; though, in the interim, expectations amongst
households and businesses and the skew of risks will shape policy decisions.
In the press conference, Chair Warsh highlighted another area of debate for the Committee: the drivers
and implications of long-term yields. Nominal and real yields have risen materially in recent months.
The strength of economic activity, particularly investment, was cited as a factor, and potentially greater
uncertainty over the FOMC’s reaction function. It seems the FOMC may be comfortable allowing the
market to adjust financial conditions to balance inflation risks without an explicit contribution from the
FOMC. The immediate response of the market to this idea was a material steepening of the yield
curve, highlighting participants’ anxiety over inflation and policy uncertainty.
Overall, it appears the FOMC is not in a rush to determine whether the current stance of policy is
appropriate to bring inflation back to target, or indeed if it is better to allow the market to balance
expectations by itself. Several months of data and discussion are likely necessary to determine the
next step based on economic data alone. However, it is entirely possible risks to inflation, or to the long
end of the yield curve, will push the FOMC to a decision sooner. Policy will be live at coming meetings,
particularly in September and October. On hold decisions are most likely, but by a very narrow margin.

Standard Chartered Bank

Charts of the week: Inflation softens

June US CPI data eases immediate inflation worries, but oil remains a risk. China, India equities have room to catch up. 
CPI inflation m/m change vs. 2yr US govt. bond yield    MSCI equity indices: Asia ex-Japan vs. China, India

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered

Editorial 
Inflation cools, but focus shifts back to oil 
Strategy summary: Peak Fed hawkishness is likely behind us 
following the release of a soft June CPI inflation print . This 
reinforces our view that the Fed will keep rates on hold through 
the second half of 2026. However, a new surge in oil prices 
poses some risks to this view. Oil prices are likely to rise to the 
top of our expected USD 70 -90/bbl near -term range, but we  
believe sufficient incentives exist for all sides to avoid a larger 
escalation that pushes prices sustainably above this range. 
We would focus on locking in attractive yields, but retain our 
strong preference for short bond maturities to avoid excessive 
exposure to any new inflation concerns. China and India 
equities should benefit within Asia from any equities broadening 
trade, alongside Taiwan equities. AI monetisation is expected 
to be a key focus for upcoming tech sector earnings.  
US inflation data signals peak hawkishness likely behind 
us: US headline CPI inflation fell -0.4% m/m, the biggest 
monthly drop in years. This supported the view that the rise in 
inflation over Q2 indeed was likely a temporary surge led by oil 
prices alone rather than being a more broad -based risk. The 
data point reinforces our view that the Fed is likely to leave rates 
unchanged through H2 2026 and signals ‘peak hawkishness’ is 
now likely behind us. Fed Chair Warsh’s inclusion of ‘full 
employment’ at his f irst semi -annual testimony to Congress 
supports this view. However, the relief was more visible in 
shorter-term US bond yields (2-year) relative to long-term bond 
yields (10-year), suggesting long term fiscal concerns remain.  
Renewed Middle East escalation poses upside oil price risk 
in the near term:  Following earlier attacks on ships transiting 
through the Strait of Hormuz, the US initiated new military 
strikes on Iran . This series of events is pushing oil prices 
towards the upper end of our expected USD 70 -90/bbl range. 
While there is a non -negligible risk that Trump follows through 
on his threats to escalate further still, we believe the US 
continues to face an incent ive to avoid a larger oil price shock 
given the risk to US growth and upcoming mid -term elections. 
This means any temporary oil price spikes above USD 90/bbl 
are unlikely to be sustained for long. Having said that, we 
continue to see US TIPS (inflation -protected bonds) as an 
attractive hedge against a more adverse scenario. 
Lock in elevated yield:  Fiscal worries notwithstanding, our 
view that we are likely past peak Fed hawkishness means that 
the recent rise in bond yields offers an opportunity to lock in 
yields for income . We maintain a broad preference for credit 
over government bonds, with an  Overweight on EM USD 
government bonds. However, we continue to favour short 
maturities to avoid excessive exposure to volatility triggered by 
any new inflation or fiscal worries. 
China, India equities to benefit from broadening of rally:  
2026 year-to-date gains in Asia ex -Japan equities have been 
driven by Korea and Taiwan equities (ie. semiconductor-heavy 
markets). Chinese and Indian equities have both lagged. 
However, these two markets should benefit from a rotation into 
broader regional exposure and a softening of the US Dollar, 
where long positioning is close to extremes. We remain 
Overweight China and India equ ities, with our preference for 
the Hang Seng tech index providing exposure to a broadening 
of gains within the Asia technology sector. 
Watch upcoming tech sector earnings, UK appointments : 
While semi-conductor sector earnings growth visibility remains 
strong, we also expect solid result announcements from US 
hyperscalers. This is likely to be driven by robust AI adoption 
and digital advertising trends. However, the key market focus is 
likely to remain on the level of AI capex spending and any 
signals on the pace of AI monetarisation.  
In the UK, the key focus for markets following Burnham’s likely 
appointment as Prime Minister will be the choice of Chancellor 
of the Exchequer and any related signals on the extent of fiscal 
policy continuity. These are likely to be key for UK bond yields  
and the GBP, which are expected to stay rangebound till 
greater clarity emerges.  
—  Manpreet Gill

Top client questions 
  What can we learn from major US banks’ Q2 earnings? 
Our view: Major US banks  delivered significant earnings beats 
in Q2 vs. consensus estimates, making them attractive to add 
on pullbacks. We maintain a Core allocation to US financials. 
Rationale: Major US banks beat consensus expectations across 
both earnings and revenues, primarily driven by a surge in equities 
trading and record investment banking fees. Market volatility, AI-
related capital markets activity and mega listings , such as  the 
SpaceX initial public offering,  supported this growth. Positive loan 
growth supported net interest income. Meanwhile, resilient US 
consumers supported the stable credit quality.  
Looking ahead, growth should be supported by a healthy pipeline 
for equity underwriting  and M&A advisory fees . An elevated 
equity market also bolsters fee income for the asset management 
business. Guidance was broadly raised , although management 
flagged tougher H2 comparisons and sounded a cautious tone  
around macro uncertaint ies, inflation risks and big fiscal deficits. 
Valuations of major US banks are elevated but justified, in our view. 
—  Michelle Kam, CFA, Investment Strategist 
  What do you expect from upcoming tech sector earnings, 
including those from US hyperscalers? 
Our view: Earnings growth in the semiconductor s ector 
remains strong with good visibility . We favour diversifying 
exposure into Taiwan equities. We expect US hyperscalers to 
deliver strong growth and prefer gaining exposure through the 
US communication services sector. 
Rationale: Earnings reports from the semiconductor industry point 
to unprecedented long-term visibility. Sustained AI-driven demand 
across both advanced logic and memory chips is giving chipmakers 
the confidence to accelerate capacity expansion plans. Taiwan 
equities’ significant exposure to the semiconductor industry makes 
them a preferred route for gaining exposure to this attractive growth. 
For US hyperscalers, we expect solid results , driven by  cloud 
growth with robust AI adoption, and healthy digital advertising trends. 
The market is likely to focus on whether hyperscalers’ significant AI 
investments are beginning to generate meaningful returns. Hence, 
management commentary on  AI monetisation  – specifically 
regarding new revenue streams through enterprise subscriptions, AI 
model usage growth and consumption-based pricing trends – will be 
critical. We also expect hyperscalers to maintain elevated AI capital 
expenditure ( capex) spending. We see the US communication 
services sector – which is dominated by large internet platforms with 
reasonable valuations – as an attractive opportunistic trade idea. 
—  Fook Hien Yap, Senior Investment Strategist

US banks are trading at a reasonable valuation 
discount to the broader US market, in line with the 
historical average, while the growth outlook 
remains strong

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 17 July 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Does the soft June US consumer inflation print validate 
the view that peak Fed hawkishness is behind us? What is your 
view on US bond yields following the soft inflation print? 
Our view: We believe peak inflation is behind us in the near term 
and expect the Fed to hold rates steady through year-end 2026. 
Short-term yields declined more than long-term yields after the 
soft June US consumer inflation print, validating our view that 
the term premium will remain elevated . We maintain our view 
that the US 2-year Treasury yield will trade in a range of 4.00-
4.25%. While the 10 -year yield has traded above our near -term 
target range of 4.25 -4.50%, we expect it to technically range 
between trend support at 4.47% and resistance at 4.63%. 
Rationale: June US headline and core inflation slowed to 3.5% 
y/y and 2.6% y/y , respectively, coming in below market 
expectations. The data supports our view that peak inflation and Fed 
hawkishness are behind us in the near term and reinforces our view 
that the Fed will remain on hold  for the rest of 2026. Despite the 
renewed Middle East tensions this week, we view the escalation as 
transient and do not expect energy prices to retest their April highs.  
Bond yields fell after the latest US inflation data, with shorter-dated 
US Treasury yields falling more than those at the long end. The 
10-year yield pulled back from its 13 July high of 4.626% to around 
4.59%, while the 2-year yield fell from its one-year high of 4.288% to 
around 4.19%. Markets now expect a less hawkish Fed. However, 
fiscal worries remain and will likely keep the term premium elevated.   
—  Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist 
  What is your  outlook for USD/CHF following the pair ’s 
attempt to recover towards 0.8150? 
Our view: We expect USD/CHF to remain rangebound with a 
bearish bias in the coming weeks, w ith the USD as the key 
driver.  
Rationale: The latest US consumer and producer inflation prints 
were softer than expected , which ha s trimmed Fed rate-hike 
expectations and weighed on the USD. Swiss inflation edged down 
to 0.5% y/y in June from 0.6% y/y . The slowdown reflected a slight 
deceleration in services prices  and some early energy price 
easing.  
We see an opportunity for  price dynamics to gradually 
reaccelerate in the coming quarters as the effect of the Swiss franc’s 
(CHF's) previous appreciation fades. This should allow the Swiss 
National Bank (SNB) to shift its focus towards policy normalisation 
over time. The central bank's increased willingness to intervene 
against excessive CHF strength  remains a latent constraint on 
USD/CHF downside, with SNB President Schlegel having recently 
reaffirmed that stance. The pair rise has been rejected repeatedly at 
0.8150 and is likely to remain rangebound with a bearish bias, with 
support at 0.7920. 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist

### Page 6

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 17 July 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Andy Burnham is widely expected to be confirmed as the 
leader of the UK Labour Party on 17 July . Will this spark 
heightened volatility in the GBP and UK bond (Gilt) yields? 
Our view: We expect Gilt yields and  the GBP to remain 
rangebound in the near term until there is greater clarity on 
Burnham’s policy agenda.  
Rationale: Andy Burnham is expected to become  Prime Minister 
(PM) of the UK on 20 July, having secured the backing of a majority 
of Labour Members of Parliament. The Gilt market is anxious about 
his appointment, as Burnham’s fiscal vision is seen as more left-
leaning than that of outgoing PM Starmer ’s. Using the 10-year Gilt 
as an example, yields have risen by about 30bps since he emerged 
as a strong contender in late June, reflecting these concerns. 
However, Burnham has publicly ruled out changing Chancellor of the 
Exchequer Rachel Reeves’s self -imposed borrowing limits, while 
signalling that he is open to reducing the welfare bill if he becomes 
PM, suggesting some degree of fiscal discipline . That said, the 
market will likely remain concerned about how Burnham may seek 
flexibility within the fiscal rules. He was reportedly considering 
bringing forward a bolstered budget later in 2026 , potentially 
combining it with the Autumn Budget, including an addit ional GBP 
40bn to fund infrastructure and housing investment, alongside the 
removal of defence spending from existing fiscal rules. Perhaps most 
worrying for the market were his comments that the UK had to 
move beyond being “in hock” to bond markets , which 
contributed to the initial yield spike when he entered the PM race. 
Having said that, we think Gilt markets have already embedded a 
meaningful near-term political risk premium. We expect the m to 
remain rangebound for now, as investors await the Autumn Budget 
and Parliament enters its summer recess until September. Whether 
Reeves retains her position in the cabinet  will also be closely 
monitored as a signal of continuity in fiscal policy. 
The larger GBP risk will come after Burnham’s confirmation, 
particularly through his choice of chancellor and the credibility of 
his spending plans . Policies that promote investment and growth 
while respecting fiscal constraints would be broadly neutral to 
positive for the GBP. In contrast, unfunded spending commitments 
or a perceived weakening of fiscal discipline are likely to  revive 
concerns over UK debt sustainability and weigh on the currency. 
Our base case is , therefore, a brief increase in GBP volatility , 
rather than a sustained directional move. GBP/USD is likely to 
remain more sensitive to the US D, US rate expectations , and 
upcoming UK fiscal announcements than to 17 July’s confirmation 
itself.  
—  Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist

Document 326: SpaceX解禁后两日暴涨23%，期权成交创纪录，空头回补或进一步推升股价

Source PDF: all report pdf/SpaceX解禁后两日暴涨23%，期权成交创纪录，空头回补或进一步推升股价.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-08-07
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

SpaceX 两⽇市值激增 3270 亿美元，股价重新逼近 135 美元 IPO 发⾏价。周五截⾄纽约时间
下午 1 时 50 分， SpaceX 期权成交量达到 224 万份合约，其中看涨期权成交量达到 130 万
份，创历史新⾼。⽬前仍有超过 2.5 亿股 SpaceX 股票被卖空，股价快速上涨，空头被迫回
补可能进⼀步推升股价。
摘要：
SpaceX 股票连续第⼆个交易⽇⼤涨，距离重新站上 135 美元的 IPO 发⾏价仅⼀步之遥。周五，公
司股价上涨约 16% ，推动两⽇累计涨幅达到约 23% ，期间市值增加超过 3270 亿美元。
这轮强势反弹发⽣在⾸批⼤规模限售股解禁之后。周四，约 9.115 亿股 SpaceX 股票解除出售限
制，可流通股数量由此前的约 6.39 亿股增⾄ 15.5 亿股，市场此前⼀度担⼼早期投资者集中抛售将
给股价带来巨⼤压⼒，但实际情况却截然相反。
SpaceX 股价的快速反弹，也令此前押注股价下跌的投资者承受巨⼤压⼒。⽬前仍有超过 2.5 亿股
SpaceX 股票被卖空，相当于当前可交易股票的约 16% 。⼀旦股价快速上涨，空头被迫回补可能

进⼀步推升股价，
与此同时， SpaceX 期权市场交易也出现异常活跃。周五截⾄纽约时间下午 1 时 50 分， SpaceX 期
权成交量达到 224 万份合约，其中看涨期权成交量达到 130 万份，创历史新⾼，显⽰资⾦正在重
新涌⼊。
不过，股价快速反弹并不意味着市场对 SpaceX ⾼估值的担忧已经消失。随着解禁带来的供给冲
击逐渐消化，投资者仍需要⾯对⼀个核⼼问题：在 SpaceX 的 AI 、卫星互联⽹和航天业务全⾯兑
现潜⼒之前，市场是否愿意继续为其⽀付极⾼的估值。
解禁不跌反涨， SpaceX 两⽇市值增加 3270 亿美元
SpaceX 此前⼀度经历剧烈回调。
公司 IPO 后不久，股价曾快速攀升⾄历史⾼位，随后累计蒸发超过 1 万亿美元市值。周三，
SpaceX 发布上市以来⾸份公开业绩报告后，股价单⽇下跌 14% ，主要原因之⼀是公司在⼈⼯智
能业务上的⽀出⾼于市场预期。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

但仅仅两个交易⽇之后，市场情绪发⽣明显逆转。
周五 SpaceX 股价上涨约 16% ，两⽇累计涨幅达到约 23% ，使股价重新逼近 135 美元的 IPO 价
格。按周五收盘价计算， SpaceX 股价报 128.18 美元。
此次反弹尤其值得注意的是，它发⽣在市场最担⼼的限售股解禁之后。
周四，约 9.115 亿股此前受到限售的股票进⼊市场，使 SpaceX 可交易股票数量从 6.39 亿股⼤幅增
加⾄ 15.5 亿股，流通股规模增加超过⼀倍。
按照市场此前的担忧，⼤量新增股票可能形成巨⼤的潜在卖压。但从实际⾛势看，解禁反⽽成
为股价反弹的起点。
空头遭遇反杀，超 2.5 亿股仍被做空
SpaceX 股价的快速反弹，也令此前押注股价下跌的投资者承受巨⼤压⼒。
根据 S3 Partners 的数据，在本轮反弹之前，空头此前累计获得的账⾯收益⼀度超过 90 亿美元。
截⾄最新数据，仍有超过 2.5 亿股 SpaceX 股票被卖空，相当于当前可交易股票的约 16%。这⼀
⽐例此前⼀度更⾼。

在 9.115 亿股股票解禁之前， SpaceX 的做空⽐例曾达到可交易股票的 36% 以上。随着⼤量新增
股票进⼊流通，做空⽐例迅速下降。
Miller Tabak ⾸席市场策略师 Matt Maley 表⽰，在解禁前后，市场上肯定存在部分看跌头⼨需要
平仓。
这意味着，本轮上涨除了受到新增买盘推动，也可能存在明显的空头回补因素。
对于⾼做空⽐例的热⻔股票⽽⾔，⼀旦股价没有按照空头预期下跌，反⽽快速上涨，空头被迫
回补可能进⼀步推升股价，从⽽形成 “ 上涨 — 回补 — 进⼀步上涨 ” 的正反馈。
期权成交创纪录，看涨资⾦开始追涨
股票现货市场之外， SpaceX 期权市场同样出现了明显的投机热潮。
彭博社汇编的交易所数据显⽰，截⾄周五纽约时间下午 1 时 50 分， SpaceX 期权成交量已经达到
224 万份合约。
其中，看涨期权成交量达到 130 万份，创历史新⾼；看跌期权成交量约为 94.3 万份。
单个合约的交易尤其活跃。 2026 年 8 ⽉ 14 ⽇到期、⾏权价 320 美元的看涨期权成交量达到 64,857

份，⽽截⾄周四，该合约的未平仓合约仅为 17,702 份。
此外， 2026 年 8 ⽉ 7 ⽇到期、⾏权价 125 美元的看跌期权成交量达到 27,866 份，对应未平仓合约
为 6,314 份； 2026 年 8 ⽉ 7 ⽇到期、⾏权价 120 美元的看涨期权成交量则达到 20,747 份，对应未平
仓合约为 26,724 份。
期权交易的急剧放⼤，尤其是看涨期权成交创下纪录，反映出部分资⾦正在押注 SpaceX 股价继
续上涨。

### Page 3

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
与此同时，SpaceX 三个⽉隐含波动率下降 1.55 个百分点⾄ 77.37% ，三个⽉ 90/110 偏度下降
1.29 个百分点⾄ -0.15 个百分点。
这表明，在股价快速反弹的同时，期权市场对尾部下跌⻛险的定价也有所缓和。
华尔街仍然看多，但⾼估值争议并未消失
尽管解禁曾被视为 SpaceX 股价的重要⻛险事件，但限售股解除并不意味着早期投资者必须⽴即
出售股票。
这也是此次市场⾛势与此前悲观预期出现明显偏差的重要原因。
与此同时，华尔街整体仍然对 SpaceX 保持⾼度乐观。
Argus Research 周五将 SpaceX 评级从 “ 持有 ” 上调⾄ “ 买⼊ ” ，理由之⼀是公司⼈⼯智能基础设施
投资可能获得 “ 快速回报 ” 。
根据彭博汇总的数据，⽬前覆盖 SpaceX 的分析师中，接近 80% 给予 “ 买⼊ ” 评级，分析师平均⽬

标价约为 221 美元。
但乐观预期背后， SpaceX 的估值仍然是市场⽆法回避的问题。
Miller Tabak ⾸席市场策略师 Matt Maley 指出，待限售股解禁带来的交易影响逐渐消退后，投资
者最终仍需要决定，是否愿意以如此⾼的价格买⼊⼀家可能需要多年才能完全兑现潜⼒的公
司。
换⾔之，眼下的反弹更多证明市场重新获得了⻛险偏好，并不意味着 SpaceX 的⻓期估值问题已
经得到解决。
AI 与太空业务，决定这场⾏情能否继续
SpaceX 当前的估值逻辑已经不再局限于传统航天业务。
公司正在同时押注⽕箭发射、卫星互联⽹以及⼈⼯智能基础设施等多个增⻓⽅向。其中， AI 投资
尤其受到资本市场关注，但也正是近期业绩报告中⽀出超预期的重要来源之⼀。
因此， SpaceX 未来股价能否继续向上突破，不仅取决于 Starlink 等现有业务的增⻓，也取决于
市场能否看到 AI 基础设施投⼊带来的实际回报。
眼下，解禁这⼀原本可能引发抛售的事件已经被市场快速消化，空头回补与看涨期权交易⼜进

⼀步放⼤了上涨动能。
但当短期交易因素逐渐退潮后，市场最终仍将回到⼀个基本问题： SpaceX 能否⽤未来数年的业
务增⻓，兑现当前已经极⾼的市场预期。
如果答案是肯定的， 135 美元可能只是下⼀轮上涨的起点；如果 AI 和航天业务的兑现速度低于预
期，那么近期由空头回补和期权资⾦推动的急涨，也可能成为新的波动源。

金価格はなぜ下落しているのか：実質金利上昇と通貨価値下落の狭間で

金価格
（右軸）
米国の実質金利
（左軸、逆目盛）
（実質金利、％、逆目盛） （金価格、㌦/㌉）
（年）
ｳｸﾗｲﾅ戦争

金価格下落は長期上昇相場の終わりか 
今年に入り、金価格は下落基調に転じた。主因は、ＦＲＢのウォ
ーシュ議長の量的引き締め（ＱＴ）志向や、原油高を起点とする
インフレ・利上げ観測に伴う米実質金利の上昇である。もっとも、
足元の下落を単なる「実質金利上昇による調整」とみるだけで
は不十分だ。2022年のウクライナ戦争以降、新興国を中心とす
るドル離れ、中央銀行による金購入、政府債務拡大に伴う法定
通貨の価値の希薄化が重なり、金の価格形成には構造変化が
生じている。金価格を実質金利で説明する回帰分析を行うと、
今年に入ってからの関係式は、過去に比べて切片（金価格の
水準）が大きく切り上がっただけでなく、傾き（実質金利に対す
る感応度）も顕著に高まっている。  
本稿では、金価格の決定メカニズムを整理し、足元の下落の背
景と下値目途、さらに長期的な見通しについて考察する。

ドル建て金価格とは「金とドルの為替レート」 
金は希少性が高く、国境を越えて価値が認められる。また、発
行体を持たず国家の信用力に依存しない。こうした点から法定
通貨を代替する決済手段としての性格を有し、「無国籍通貨」と
も呼ばれる。  
金を通貨と捉えれば、ドル建ての金価格は、ドルと金の為替
（交換）レートとみることができる。一般に為替レートは二国間
の実質金利差の影響を受けるが、金には利子も配当もなく、実
質金利はゼロだ。それゆえ、ドル建て金価格は米国の実質金
利そのものと強い連動性を持つ。実際、2000 年代以降の金相
場の大部分は、米実質金利との逆相関で説明できた（図表１）。  
しかし、2022 年以降、この関係は崩れた。ＦＲＢの利上げで米
実質金利が急上昇したが、金価格は下がらず上昇を続けた。
ウクライナ戦争や中東情勢の緊迫化により、安全資産需要、い
わゆる「有事の金買い」が強まったためだ。

図表1：米国の実質金利と金価格の連動性

注：実質金利＝米10年国債利回り‐米BEI10年 
  2007 年～21年の金価格の動きの8 割超は実質金利で説明できた 
出所：Bloomberg、ソニーFG

ディベースメント取引とは何か 
これに加えて、近年の金価格を理解する上で欠かせないのが、
ディベースメント（Debasement）という視点だ。ディベースメント
とは、財政赤字の拡大や政府債務の膨張、金融緩和や通貨供
給量の増大を背景に、法定通貨の価値が希薄化することを指
す。ディベースメント取引とは、こうした通貨価値下落のヘッジ
として、供給量に限りのある金を価値保蔵手段として保有する
投資行動である。コロナ禍に見舞われた2020 年以降、拡張財
政と金融緩和が世界的な潮流となるなかで、この種の取引が
活発化し、金価格を押し上げた。

金価格の決定要因の変遷 
あらためて過去 20 年間の金価格の決定要因を振り返ると、以
下の３局面に整理できる。  
① 2007年～21 年： 実質金利相場 
この時期は金が「無国籍通貨」と認識され、金価格はドル価値
の裏返しとして、米国の実質金利でほぼ説明できた。米実質金
利低下とドル安は金高要因、実質金利上昇とドル高は金安要
因となった（前掲図表１）。 
 金は無国籍通貨であり、ドル建て 金価格とは金とドルの為替レート 。為替レートは二国間の実質金利差で
動くが、金には金利が付かないことから、金価格は米国の実質金利と強い逆相関を持つ 
 しかし、 ウクライナ戦争 後は金が安全資産・ 準備資産としての位置づけを強め、金価格の決定 要因が変
化。ドル離れや中銀の金購入、通貨価値の希薄化をテーマに、金価格は実質金利から乖離して高騰した 
 今年に入り、金価格と実質金利の連動性が復活 。ウォーシュＦＲＢ議長のＱＴ （通貨価値の回復）志向と 、
原油高・利上げ期待に伴う米国の実質金利上昇が金価格を下押し 
 当面は循環的な実質金利の上昇が金価格 を抑える一方、長期的には通貨価値の希薄化という構造 要因
が金価格を押し上げる公算 
KEY 
POINT

①2007年～21年
② 2022年～25 年： 「有事の金」相場 
2022 年のウクライナ戦争は金市場の構造を変えた。米国によ
るロシア資産の凍結は、各国にドル資産の制裁リスク（ドルの
武器化）を意識させた。中国・中東諸国・グローバルサウスを中
心に外貨準備の多様化（ドル離れ）が進み、各国中銀は米国債
に代わる準備資産として金を積み増した（図表２）。コロナ禍以
降のディベースメント取引に、有事の金買いと準備資産需要が
加わり、金価格は実質金利では説明できない高騰をみせた。

図表２：外貨準備の構成変化～米国債売りと金買い増し

出所：IMF、CMX、Bloomberg、ソニーFG

③ 2026年～： 実質金利相場2.0 の到来 
今年に入ると状況は急変し、金価格は再び実質金利との連動
性を強めている（図表３）。転換点は、１月31 日のケビン・ウォ
ーシュ氏のＦＲＢ議長指名と、２月末以降のイラン攻撃である。
これらを機に、金市場は再び実質金利、金融政策、ドル価値を
重視し始めた。

図表３：2026 年以降、金価格は再び実質金利に連動

出所：Bloomberg、ソニーFG

金価格を押し下げる実質金利相場2.0 とは 
現在の金価格下落は次の３つの要因で説明できる。 
第一に量的引き締め（ＱＴ）観測である。ウォーシュＦＲＢ議長は
量的緩和の副作用に批判的で、ＱＴを重視している。市場はこ
れを、過剰流動性の是正を通じた通貨価値の回復策と受け止
めており、ディベースメント取引の巻き戻しが金価格を押し下げ
ている。 
さらに、ＱＴが米国債のタームプレミアムや実質金利を押し上げ
れば、これも金価格の下押し圧力となる（図表４）。

図表４：ＦＲＢの保有資産の圧縮とタームプレミアムの上昇

注：米国債保有比率＝FRBの米国債保有額／米国債残高 
出所：FRB、Bloomberg、ソニーFG

第二に、市場参加者の着眼点の変化である。通常、原油高や
インフレ懸念の高まりは、インフレヘッジ需要を通じて金価格を
押し上げる。しかし足元では、金市場の焦点が再び実質金利
へ移るなか、投資家はインフレそのものよりも、それに伴う金
融引き締めや実質金利上昇を意識している。その結果、「イン
フレ懸念→利上げ観測→実質金利上昇→金価格下落」という
経路が優勢となり、原油高にもかかわらず金価格は下落して
いる。  
第三に、中銀による金購入の鈍化である。中銀買いは、依然と
して高水準ながら、今年に入り頭打ち感が出ている（前掲
図表
２）。その結果、需給要因よりも実質金利要因が金相場を左右
しやすくなっている。

回帰線の変化は何を意味するのか 
2007年～21 年（上記①）と26 年以降（③）は、どちらも実質金利
相場だが、実質金利と金価格の関係性には大きな変化が見ら
れる。注目すべきは、回帰式の「切片の上昇」と「傾きの急勾配
化」である（図表５）。

図表５：実質金利と金価格の散布図（切片上昇と急勾配化）

出所：Bloomberg、ソニーFG

ﾆｸｿﾝｼｮｯｸ 金価格
（右軸）
米国の政府債務残高
（点線は予想、左軸）
（金価格、㌦/㌉）
（年）
切片の上昇は、同じ実質金利でも金価格の水準が高くなったこ
とを意味する。背景には、安全資産・準備資産としての金需要の
高まりや、政府債務増大に伴う通貨価値の希薄化など、2022～
25 年に生じた構造変化がある。 
一方、傾きの急勾配化は、実質金利の変化に対する金価格の
感応度が高まったことを意味する。では、なぜ2026 年以降の
金価格は、2007～21 年よりも実質金利に敏感になったのか。  
一つの仮説は、金がこれまで以上に通貨としての性格を強めて
いることである。従来、実質金利の上昇は、金利の付かない金
の保有コストを高める「機会費用効果」を通じて金価格を押し下
げてきた。現在はこれに加え、ウォーシュ議長のＱＴ志向がドル
価値の回復期待を高め、ディベースメント取引を巻き戻している
ことが追い打ちをかけている。

金価格の下値目途は3,600～3,800 ドル 
今後の金の下値目途を考える上での焦点は、実質金利の上昇
余地である。現在の実質金利は、利上げ期待の高まりを受けて
2.3
％まで上昇し、米国の潜在成長率に到達している（図表６）。
通常、実質金利が潜在成長率を上回れば景気が悪化し、それ
が金利の低下圧力となる。その点で実質金利の上昇余地は大
きくない。また、現在の長期金利は先物市場が予想する１％超
もの追加利上げを織り込み済みであり、この点でも実質金利の
上昇余地は限られる。  
現在の回帰式（金価格＝−1905×実質金利＋8430）を前提とす
ると、実質金利が2.4％まで上昇した場合の金の下値は3,800ド
ル程度、2.5％では3,600ドル程度と試算される。

図表６：実質金利は潜在成長率に到達、さらなる上昇余地は小

注：シャドーは景気後退局面 
出所：Bloomberg、ソニーFG

短期は逆風、長期は構造的な追い風 
短期的には、金市場を取り巻く環境は厳しい。利上げ期待によ
る実質金利の上昇と、ＱＴ観測によるディベースメント取引の巻
き戻しは、いずれも金価格の逆風となる。  
一方、長期的には金価格を強気にみる余地が残る。コロナ禍
以降の世界的なインフレは国民の不満を高め、政治家をポピュ
リズム政策に傾斜させている。その結果、財政拡張と政府債務
の膨張は世界的な潮流となり（図表７）、それに伴う通貨供給量
の拡大は、ＱＴ要因を跳ね返し、金価格を構造的に押し上げる
要因となっている。

図表７：ポピュリズムへの傾斜と政府債務の膨張は世界の潮流

出所：IMF、ソニーFG

法定通貨の増発には制限がない一方、金の供給量には限りが
ある。そのため、通貨価値の希薄化は金の相対的な希少性を
高める。図表８の通り、米国の政府債務残高は長期的に増加し、
金価格もこれに連動する形で上昇してきた。米議会予算局（ＣＢ
Ｏ）の試算によれば、2036 年には米国の政府債務残高が56 兆
ドルに達する見通しである。これは現在の約２倍に当たり、金
価格の上昇余地がなお大きいことを示唆している。

図表８：政府債務の膨張は長期的な金価格の上昇を示唆

注：政府債務残高の予想は米議会予算局。1971年のニクソンショックで金本位制
が崩壊すると、通貨発行の制約が解かれ、政府債務が増加した 
出所：米議会予算局、Bloomberg、ソニーFG

金価格は循環的な調整局面にあるが、 
構造的な上昇トレンドは崩れず 
金価格は 2022 年～25 年に形成された高い均衡水準を維持し
たまま、市場の焦点は再び実質金利へと戻りつつある。足元は
「有事の金」相場から「実質金利相場2.0」への移行局面と位置
付けられよう。当面は実質金利の上昇やＱＴ観測が金の上値を
抑える一方、構造的には通貨価値の希薄化が金価格を下支え
しよう。足元の金価格下落は長期トレンドの終焉ではなく、長期
上昇相場の中の循環的な調整局面とみるのが妥当だろう。 
渡辺 浩志

 , Spain

 .

 , and  
i

The

sensitivity at a record high (+1 std. dev. in the PFI adds +53

premium

 . Note that

out redenomination

argues for a wider hedge around their electoral and budget calendar, while 
 risk premia  
from consensus system

 (e.g. Netherlands) tend to stay anchored through political 
noise.

Political fragility is a major yet hidden factor for pricing European government bonds  
With upcoming elections in France (spring 2027) and Italy (latest in December 2027), political risk will 
be at the center of European government 
 b
ond markets.  
 But it is notoriously difficult to measure
 . 
Political risk has 
 played an important role in  
European government bond 
 prices 
since the euro crisis  
of 
2012
, influencing  
 them in two way
 s
: 
 Political risk  
 can 
 amplif
 y 
 or 
 dampen  
 the risk premia implied by 
economic and fiscal fundamentals 
 depending 
 on 
whether 
the 
political environment 
 is 
perceived as fragile  
or stable
 , and it 
can 
also 
 reflect 
the probability of an 
 intentional 
 Euro 
exit
. This so
-
called “redenomination 
risk” 
is closely related to the  
fragmentation  
of 
 Europe’s 
 political systems under the ascent of populist
 , 
euro
-
sceptic 
 movements.  
 But 
 political risk 
 is 
 notoriously difficult to measure
 , especially on 
 a 
 market
 -
relevant 
 frequency
 , and often 
 remains 
 under 
qualitative  
assessment  
prone to 
 personal  
bias

. 
To 
bring 
this hidden factor into the light  
of 
objective 
measurement
 , we 
develop  
a high
 -
frequency index 
 to 
quantify 
the impact of political fragility on 
 European government bond prices
 , 
and ultimately set a 
 fiscal 
 price 
tag  
on 
political fragility in Europe.   
Our  
 Political Fragility Index 
 (PFI) 
 turns a country's politics into a single weekly number. 
 Our base 
assumption is that we can observe the risk 
 inherent in 
political system
 s 
by observ

 dynamics within 
the 
party system. 
 This is 
a 
crystallization of 
 the 
underlying 
 social 
 cleavages  
and 
 can 
 therefore 
serve 
as a 
proxy for the political system as a whole
 , using 
weekly 
 aggregation
 s 
of 
election 
polls
 , which 
are available 
for 
almost all EU countries plus the UK
 . In 
th
is 
analysis
 , 
we will focus on eight countries: 
 France, Germany, 
Italy, Spain, the UK, Belgium, the Netherlands and Austria
 . 
We leave out Portugal and Greece, whose 
bailout and debt
 -

 financing  
dynamic  
and, with them, our results.

Sovereign risk premia 
 have never been more sensitive to political fragility  
T
he translation of this political fragility to European Government Bond risk premia is anything but 
mechanical.  
Fragility can remain latent for years without leaving a clear imprint on sovereign debt prices. 
On the other hand, risk premia can reprice strongly on news flow even though the underlying political 
fragility remained unchanged. 
 To measure

 as 
marginal driver of

effect

and mostly negative

European government bonds are pricing out euro  
 break
 -
up risk almost entirely, despite a rise in 
Eurosceptic votes
 . 
The risk premium on 
 Eurozone  
government bonds embeds two distinct risks: a fiscal 
risk tied to economic fundamentals and a "redenomination risk" reflecting the probability of a country 
exiting the euro, whether by political intent or market accident. By decomposing the risk premium i
 nto 
these two components and examining 
 to which extent they  
respond to changes in the PFI Index, we can 
establish whether markets price political fragility primarily as a fiscal risk or as a 
 e
uro 
break
 -
up 
risk. 
The 
result 
is clear. 
 Before  
the ECB 
 launched  
QE in 2015
 , redenomination  
risk was 
 present  
and accounted for 
almost half of the spread sensitivity 
 for 
Italy and Spain
 . 
Today, sensitivity is driven almost entirely by fiscal 
risk. Redenomination risk has 
 totally  
disappeared since QE, suggesting the emergency mechanisms put 
in place by the ECB and the EU are viewed as credible
 ; 
an exit by market accident is no longer seen as 
plausible. An intentional exit, whether through a Eurosceptic populist government or a referendum, 
remains a tail risk. But it is one  
the market no longer appears to price
 . 
The 
Eurosceptic  
risk seems to have 
shifted entirely from a 
 mix of fiscal and  
implied currency risk 
 to a risk of 
 populist 
 fiscal profligacy (non 
respect of 
 domestic 
 and 
 European budget rules
 ) (Figure 
 5
)
.
